9 de noviembre de 2026 al 7 de febrero de 2027

Contenidos por marca

Una pestaña por marca. En K&G Market y Klean NYC, «Calendario» es el plan de 3 meses en español, y «Overview» y «Week 1» a «Week 4» son los posts de 4 semanas listos para copiar (en inglés), con guiones, prompts y TikToks. JDET Capital y Klean Perú tienen su calendario.

Resumen

Fases, semanas, revisiones, reglas de ajuste y fechas clave de las cuatro marcas.

Cómo está armado

Las cuatro primeras semanas se detallan día por día porque ya hay datos de partida. Las otras nueve se planifican por tema. En cada revisión se consulta Metricool, se compara con la línea base y se detalla el siguiente bloque. Planificar las 13 semanas pieza por pieza sería adelantarse a datos que todavía no existen.

Base · semanas 1 a 4

9 de noviembre al 6 de diciembre. Instalar el ritmo, ordenar la identidad de cada marca y medir. Incluye Thanksgiving.

Temporada · semanas 5 a 8

7 de diciembre al 3 de enero. Navidad, fin de año y Año Nuevo, con una semana baja.

Consolidación · semanas 9 a 13

4 de enero al 7 de febrero. Repetir lo que funcionó y preparar el siguiente trimestre.

Puntos de revisión

FechaQué se revisaQué se decide
Vie 20 novCumplimiento del ritmo, impresiones por publicación y primeras lecturas de formato y hora.Ajustar horarios. Mantener el plan o bajar al mínimo.
Vie 4 dicCuatro semanas contra la línea base: seguidores, impresiones, Reel, clics en Google y LinkedIn.Resultado de la ventana de Instagram, formatos a repetir y detalle de las semanas 5 a 8.
Vie 18 dicTemporada de fiestas: alcance y mensajes.Detalle de enero (semanas 9 a 12) y ritmo de la semana baja.
Vie 29 eneTrimestre completo contra las metas propuestas.Plan de febrero a abril.

En cada fecha basta con pedir la revisión: se consultan los datos de Metricool y se actualiza este calendario.

Reglas de ajuste

Umbrales propuestos, para confirmar con los primeros datos.

  • Reel de K&G Market. Si supera 150 vistas dos semanas seguidas, pasar de uno a dos Reels por semana.
  • Reels de Klean N Green NYC. En la revisión 1 se comparan con los carruseles del miércoles. Si tienen menos alcance, el viernes pasa a imagen única.
  • Horario de Instagram. Si la ventana de 18:00 a 20:00 supera en impresiones a la de 10:00 a 12:00 durante dos semanas, mover Instagram a la tarde.
  • LinkedIn con buen resultado. Si una publicación supera 50 impresiones, repetir el tema con otro formato en las dos semanas siguientes.
  • LinkedIn con bajo alcance. Si sigue bajo 15 impresiones por publicación tras cuatro semanas, reforzar conexiones y compartidos desde perfiles personales antes de aumentar el ritmo.
  • TikTok. Las cuentas son nuevas y no hay datos propios: los horarios de martes y jueves a las 18:00 vienen de la referencia general de Metricool. En la revisión 1 se comparan vistas y reproducción completa entre martes y jueves y entre temas; se repite el tipo de tema que supere a los demás y se mueve el horario si otra franja rinde más.
  • Capacidad del equipo. Si se cumple menos del 80% de lo programado dos semanas seguidas, volver al plan mínimo.

Overview

Texts, image prompts, Reels and hashtags for K&G Market over four weeks.

What is ready and what is missing

K&G Market, Monday, November 9 to Sunday, December 6, 2026. All times are New York time.

21pieces over four weeks: 8 for Instagram and Facebook (photos, a carousel, single images), 4 Reels, 4 Google posts, 4 Saturday Story sets and one optional Thanksgiving StoryData
4Reels, each with a continuity block, 3 Seedance scene prompts, a voiceover with Eleven v4 tags, on-screen text and captionsData
4Google Business Profile posts, one every Monday, with text, button and linkData
13image prompts for product photos, notices, the carousel and the four Google posts, all without text and without peopleData
36drafts in Metricool for K&G Market (Instagram, Facebook, Google and TikTok), none published; they still need the real images, the bracket details and the videos for the Reels and TikToksData
8TikToks of about 18 seconds, each with a different AI presenter, a script, a video prompt and a captionData

Next step. All texts are written in English with a Spanish line, as the brand voice defines. What is left is the real material: opening hours, the products behind each bracket, the team photo, and the Saturday Stories on the day. Nothing is published without approval.

Pieces and sizes

PieceSizeNotes
Photo and single image1080 x 1350 px (4:5)Instagram and Facebook. Real photo first; the generated image is the alternative. No text on the generated image: the key details are added in Canva.
Carousel (Nov 23)1080 x 1350 px (4:5)3 images, one product per image.
Reel1080 x 1920 px (9:16)About 12 seconds, 3 Seedance scenes of about 4 seconds, hands only. Wednesdays, Instagram and Facebook.
Stories1080 x 1920 px (9:16)Saturdays, 3 frames shot that day with the phone. Keep key content out of the top and bottom 250 px.
Google Business Profile1200 x 900 px (4:3)Mondays. A real photo is better; the generated image is the alternative. No phone number in the text.

Look of the images

  • Photorealistic, soft daylight with a warm tone, wood, linen and white ceramic, so the produce color carries the image.
  • No text and no people in generated images. Text goes on in Canva; people appear only in real photos, with their permission.
  • Bottom-left corner empty for the logo, as in the Klean N Green NYC pack.
  • Stories are not generated: they are shot that day with the phone.

Video prompts for Seedance

Every Reel in this pack follows the same settings.

  • Aspect ratio 9:16 (vertical), the highest resolution the tool offers (1080 x 1920 px or above).
  • 4 seconds per scene, 3 scenes, about 12 seconds in total. Use 5 seconds per scene if you want more room to trim when editing.
  • Sound: ambient only (pouring, sizzling, simmering). No music and no voice inside Seedance; add the voiceover (each Reel card has its script) and licensed music in the video editor.
  • Paste the continuity block at the start of every scene prompt, so the kitchen, the light, the props and the hands stay the same from scene to scene.
  • Generate and judge one scene at a time. If the tool offers it, use the last frame of the previous scene as the reference for the next one.
  • Change one line at a time: if the movement is right but the framing is off, edit only the Camera line; if the framing is right but the movement is off, edit only Subject and action.

Before approving

  • Opening hours. Every text has [opening hours]. Send the regular hours and the special ones for Nov 26, Nov 27, Dec 24, Dec 25, Dec 31 and Jan 1.
  • Products. The brackets for the Caribbean product (Nov 16), the three Thanksgiving products (Nov 23) and the restocked product (Nov 30) need real items. Ají amarillo and chicha morada already appear in the account's own posts.
  • Dishes in the Reels. Rice and peas (Nov 18) and sweet plantains (Nov 25) are proposals for the [Caribbean dish] and [dish] in the calendar. Confirm that the store sells the ingredients, or give another dish and the prompts are rewritten.
  • Purple corn (Dec 2). The Reel assumes the store sells purple corn. If it sells ready-made chicha morada, the steps change to "shake, chill and pour".
  • Pre-orders. Thanksgiving and Christmas posts mention [date, if offered]. If the store does not take orders ahead, delete those lines.
  • Phone number. No text includes it. The website and the Google profile show +1 347-365-9656 and a Facebook extract shows 347-739-5457: confirm which one is correct before adding it anywhere.
  • Website. The Google button points to kngmarket.com, which does not appear in Google yet (see the SEO review). If you prefer, use the Call now button once the phone is confirmed.
  • Hashtag #KGMarket. Check on Instagram that no other business uses it. The Small Business Saturday and Christmas posts add #SmallBusinessSaturday and #ChristmasFood to the calendar's sets.
  • Team photo (Nov 13). Needs the team's permission and their names. Never use a generated image for that post.
  • Spanish lines. They are written in neutral Spanish. Tell me if the store prefers Peruvian or Caribbean terms for any product.
  • Colors and logo. No palette or fonts were provided for K&G Market, so the prompts use natural food colors and leave the bottom-left corner empty for your logo. If you share the logo and colors, the Canva overlays can match.
  • Metricool. The 36 drafts (28 for Instagram, Facebook and Google, plus 8 for TikTok) are created for K&G Market and none is published. Before scheduling each one, in the editor: replace the Instagram placeholder image with the real one, fill in every bracket, switch the four Instagram and Facebook Reels to Reel and upload the video, add the Brooklyn location on Instagram and Facebook, add the image and button on the Google posts, and on TikTok replace the placeholder photo with the video and switch on the AI-generated content label.

TikTok with AI presenters

Posting times come from Metricool's general TikTok reference, because the account is new and has no history yet.

Format. Eight TikToks for K&G Market (@kngmarket), two per week, Tuesday and Thursday at 6 PM New York time (week 3 moves for Thanksgiving). Each one is a video of about 18 seconds in which a different AI-generated presenter talks to the camera. The topics are product and produce tips: how to choose, how to use, what to cook. The scripts, prompts and captions are inside each week, among the other pieces.

8TikToks for K&G Market, each with its own presenter, script, video prompt and captionData
8drafts saved in Metricool, with a placeholder photo until you add the videoData
18 sabout, per video: 3 beats of 35 to 47 wordsData

TikTok: how each video is made

  • 1. Generate. Copy the video prompt in Seedance or a similar tool. If it does not produce a voice with accurate lip sync, record the script in ElevenLabs and apply lip sync to the video.
  • 2. Clips. If your generator caps a clip below 18 seconds, generate beats 1 and 2 as one clip and beat 3 as another, with the same presenter and setting, and join them in the editor.
  • 3. Check. The face, hands and mouth look natural, the words match the script, and nothing on screen is garbled. Regenerate otherwise.
  • 4. Edit. Add the on-screen text and subtitles in the TikTok or video editor. Generators garble text inside video, so the prompts ask for none.
  • 5. Upload. Open the Metricool draft, remove the placeholder photo, upload the video and switch on the AI-generated content label.
  • 6. Schedule. Only after you have checked it. Nothing here is published without your approval.

TikTok: presenter rules

  • A different presenter in every video. Different ages, skin tones and settings. No presenter returns, and none should resemble a real person.
  • The presenter is a host, not a customer or an employee. The scripts give tips and information. None says "I shop here" or "I have been cleaning for years", because an invented person cannot give a real testimonial.
  • Always labeled as AI. Every caption ends with "AI-generated video." and the TikTok AI-generated content label must be on when the video is uploaded.
  • Avoid in every prompt: subtitles, logos, watermarks, music, extra people, readable brand names and text on screen.

TikTok: before approving

  • Products. Every video mentions products the store should have on that date. Confirm stock before scheduling, or change the line. The scripts say "ask us" where stock is not certain.
  • Hours. The captions of Nov 24 and Nov 25 contain [hours]. Fill them in or delete the line.
  • Thursday, November 26. It is Thanksgiving, so there is no TikTok that day. Week 3 moves to Tuesday and Wednesday.
  • Facts. The produce tips are general and common (ripeness, storage, safe cooking of yuca). If the store prefers different advice, change the script.
  • Spanish. The scripts are in English, with one short Spanish line in the first video. The captions carry the Spanish. Tell me if the store wants more Spanish in the voice.
  • People in images. The image rules for K&G Market posts (no generated people) do not change. These AI presenters exist only in the TikTok videos.
  • Commercial content label. Metricool left TikTok's "your brand" option off. Decide when scheduling whether to turn it on.

Spoken script (3 beats)
This is ají amarillo, the yellow pepper behind Peruvian flavor.
It's bright and fruity with a medium heat, and it's the base of huancaína sauce and ají de gallina.
Ask for it at K&G Market in Brooklyn. Pásate por la tienda.
On-screen text for the first 3 seconds (add in the editor)
The yellow pepper behind Peruvian flavor
Video prompt (Seedance or similar)
Vertical 9:16 smartphone-style video, about 18 seconds, one continuous take. Woman in her 60s, short silver hair, warm brown skin, denim apron speaks directly to the camera in a bright home kitchen with a wooden counter and a small bowl of yellow pepper paste next to a cutting board. Natural, friendly, conversational delivery with small hand gestures, chest-up handheld selfie framing, soft natural light, realistic skin and hair, accurate lip sync. Spoken dialogue in English: "This is ají amarillo, the yellow pepper behind Peruvian flavor. It's bright and fruity with a medium heat, and it's the base of huancaína sauce and ají de gallina. Ask for it at K&G Market in Brooklyn. Pásate por la tienda." No on-screen text, no subtitles, no logos, no watermark, no music. A realistic fictional person, not the likeness of any real person.

Instagram and Facebook · Reel, about 12 seconds Text ready · add video

  • Drafts created in Metricool for K&G Market (Instagram and Facebook), New York time, with the caption and hashtags. Nothing is published. Instagram holds a placeholder image and Facebook is text only: in the editor, switch each one to Reel and upload the video.
  • Reels are the format that reached the most people on this account (the August Reel got 136 views), so this is the weekly anchor.
What the video needs
  • Vertical 9:16, 1080 x 1920 px. 3 scenes of 4 seconds each, about 12 seconds in total.
  • If the store can film the real dish, a real video beats a generated one. The prompts below are for when it cannot.
  • Add the on-screen text, the voiceover (script below) and licensed music in the video editor: generators garble text inside video.
  • Cover: use a frame from scene 1 and add the title in Canva.
  • The settings and the way the prompts are built are explained in the Overview, under "Video prompts for Seedance".

On-screen text (add in the video editor)
Scene 1: 3 ways to use ají amarillo · 1. Huancaína
Scene 2: 2. Ají de gallina
Scene 3: 3. Ceviche
Voiceover script (audio)
  • About 23 words for 12 seconds: relaxed pace, with a short pause between scenes. If it feels rushed, drop the first sentence of the scene.
  • The first line is the hook: say it like a question to a friend, within the first two seconds. Smile on the last line, the call to action.
  • Tone: talk, don't read. Like telling a friend something you just noticed, in casual American English, with contractions and a little humor.
Voiceover by scene, with timing
0-4 s  Scene 1: Ají amarillo, three ways. One: huancaína over potatoes.
4-8 s  Scene 2: Two: ají de gallina, creamy and warm.
8-12 s  Scene 3: Three: ceviche! Find the ají at K&G Market.
  • Model: Eleven v4. The tags in square brackets are directions, not spoken text. Paste the whole block as it is.
  • A tag holds until the next one, so tags change only where the delivery changes. One emotion per phrase works best.
  • Generate the three lines as one take so the tone stays continuous, then cut it to the scenes in the video editor.
  • If a line does not land, change the tag instead of rewriting the words: [warmly], [excited], [curious], [softly], [chuckles], [short pause]. Capitals mark emphasis, and "..." a longer beat.
  • Voice matters as much as the text: pick a warm, natural American voice before iterating. Keep the phone number and web address out of the voiceover.
Script with audio tags for Eleven v4 (paste into ElevenLabs)
[curious] Ají amarillo, three ways. [warmly] One: huancaína over potatoes.
[warmly] Two: ají de gallina, creamy and warm.
[excited] Three: ceviche! Find the ají at K&G Market.
Plain script, without tags, to record yourself
Ají amarillo, three ways. One: huancaína over potatoes. Two: ají de gallina, creamy and warm. Three: ceviche! Find the ají at K&G Market.

Stories: store open, product of the week, hours

Instagram and Facebook · 3 Stories Shoot on the day

What to shoot and what to write
  • Vertical 9:16, 1080 x 1920 px. Real photos or short clips taken that day with the phone: Stories are about what is happening now, so they are not generated.
  • Keep the key content away from the top and bottom 250 px, where the app draws its own controls.
  • Add the text with the app's text tool (short, one line per language), plus a Location sticker for K&G Market on the first frame.
  • These stories keep the same structure every Saturday; only the product of the week changes.
  1. Frame 1. Shoot: The store entrance or the counter, open and bright.

TikTok: Rice and peas: keep the scotch bonnet whole

TikTok @kngmarket · AI presenter video, about 18 s Draft · add video

Script, on-screen text and video prompt
Spoken script (3 beats)
Making rice and peas? Don't cut the scotch bonnet.
Add it whole and it gives flavor without the burn. Cut it, and the heat takes over. Take it out before serving, and wash your hands after touching it.
Find the ingredients for the pot at K&G Market.
On-screen text for the first 3 seconds (add in the editor)
Rice and peas: don't cut the scotch bonnet
Video prompt (Seedance or similar)
Vertical 9:16 smartphone-style video, about 18 seconds, one continuous take. Woman in her 20s, long braids, dark brown skin, yellow hoodie speaks directly to the camera in a small apartment kitchen, a pot on the stove and one whole orange scotch bonnet on the counter. On-screen text by scene
On-screen text (add in the video editor)
Scene 1: Rice and peas in 3 steps · 1. Simmer the beans
Scene 2: 2. Add the rice and one whole scotch bonnet
Scene 3: 3. Cover, steam, fluff
Voiceover script (audio)
  • About 28 words for 12 seconds: relaxed pace, with a short pause between scenes. If it feels rushed, drop the first sentence of the scene.
  • The first line is the hook: say it like a question to a friend, within the first two seconds. Smile on the last line, the call to action.
  • Tone: talk, don't read. Like telling a friend something you just noticed, in casual American English, with contractions and a little humor.
Voiceover by scene, with timing
0-4 s  Scene 1: Rice and peas in three steps. One: simmer the beans.
4-8 s  Scene 2: Two: add the rice and one whole scotch bonnet.
8-12 s  Scene 3: Three: cover, steam, fluff. It's all at K&G Market!
  • Model: Eleven v4. The tags in square brackets are directions, not spoken text. Paste the whole block as it is.
  • A tag holds until the next one, so tags change only where the delivery changes. One emotion per phrase works best.
  • Generate the three lines as one take so the tone stays continuous, then cut it to the scenes in the video editor.
  • If a line does not land, change the tag instead of rewriting the words: [warmly], [excited], [curious], [softly], [chuckles], [short pause]. Capitals mark emphasis, and "..." a longer beat.
  • Voice matters as much as the text: pick a warm, natural American voice before iterating. Keep the phone number and web address out of the voiceover.
Script with audio tags for Eleven v4 (paste into ElevenLabs)
[curious] Rice and peas in three steps. One: simmer the beans.
[warmly] Two: add the rice and one whole scotch bonnet.
[excited] Three: cover, steam, fluff. It's all at K&G Market!

What to shoot and what to write
  • Vertical 9:16, 1080 x 1920 px. Real photos or short clips taken that day with the phone: Stories are about what is happening now, so they are not generated.

  • If the store does not take pre-orders, replace frame 2 with a second product and delete the order line from the Friday post.

On-screen text (add in the video editor)
Scene 1: Sweet plantains for Thanksgiving · 1. Slice
Scene 2: 2. Fry until golden
Scene 3: 3. Serve warm
Voiceover script (audio)
  • About 23 words for 12 seconds: relaxed pace, with a short pause between scenes. If it feels rushed, drop the first sentence of the scene.
  • The first line is the hook: say it like a question to a friend, within the first two seconds. Smile on the last line, the call to action.
  • Tone: talk, don't read. Like telling a friend something you just noticed, in casual American English, with contractions and a little humor.
Voiceover by scene, with timing
0-4 s  Scene 1: Sweet plantains for your Thanksgiving table. One: slice ripe plantains.
4-8 s  Scene 2: Two: fry until deep golden.
8-12 s  Scene 3: Three: serve warm! Get them at K&G Market.
  • Model: Eleven v4. The tags in square brackets are directions, not spoken text. Paste the whole block as it is.
  • A tag holds until the next one, so tags change only where the delivery changes. One emotion per phrase works best.
  • Generate the three lines as one take so the tone stays continuous, then cut it to the scenes in the video editor.
  • If a line does not land, change the tag instead of rewriting the words: [warmly], [excited], [curious], [softly], [chuckles], [short pause]. Capitals mark emphasis, and "..." a longer beat.
  • Voice matters as much as the text: pick a warm, natural American voice before iterating. Keep the phone number and web address out of the voiceover.

[warmly] Two: fry until deep golden.
[excited] Three: serve warm! Get them at K&G Market.

Spoken script (3 beats)
How to pick a sweet pineapple.
Smell the bottom: it should smell sweet, not sour. Look for green leaves and a fruit that feels heavy. And keep the peel: it's the base of chicha morada.
Ask us at K&G Market.
On-screen text for the first 3 seconds (add in the editor)
Pick a sweet pineapple in 3 seconds
Video prompt (Seedance or similar)

Natural, friendly, conversational delivery with small hand gestures, chest-up handheld selfie framing, soft natural light, realistic skin and hair, accurate lip sync. And keep the peel: it's the base of chicha morada. Ask us at K&G Market." No on-screen text, no subtitles, no logos, no watermark, no music. Script, on-screen text and video prompt
Spoken script (3 beats)
How to pick yuca.
Choose firm roots with no soft spots or dark streaks. The flesh should be bright white. Always cook it, never eat it raw.
Ask for yuca at K&G Market. Boil it, then fry or mash it.

Klean N Green NYC

Servicios de limpieza para hogares, oficinas y administración de propiedades en Nueva York.

Estrategia de contenidos: tiempo recuperado

Mensaje central. Contratar a Klean N Green NYC da tiempo para hacer lo que antes no se podía. Cada pieza muestra qué puede hacer la persona con ese tiempo (unas 2 de cada 3) o es un consejo útil que termina con un puente al servicio (1 de cada 3). Ninguna promete horas ahorradas ni cuenta lo que hicieron clientes reales con su tiempo.

25 de 38piezas muestran el tiempo recuperado (la mezcla ronda 2 de cada 3)Dato
13 de 38piezas siguen como consejo y cierran con un puente al servicioDato
15piezas ya programadas con sus medios: solo cambia el textoDato
23borradores: texto nuevo y, donde cambió la idea, escenas, guiones y prompts nuevosDato

Aprobación. Nada en Metricool ha cambiado. Cuando apruebes cada pieza, se actualiza: las programadas se editan en su lugar (conservan hora y medios) y los borradores se reemplazan con el texto nuevo. Una pieza sin aprobar queda como está.

Semanas 1 a 4: día por día (hora de Nueva York)

Ritmo. Plan completo de 7 días: lunes Google, martes LinkedIn, miércoles Instagram y Facebook (carrusel), jueves LinkedIn, viernes Reel, sábado imagen única en Instagram y Facebook, domingo Instagram. A eso se suman dos TikToks por semana con presentadores IA a las 18:00 (martes y jueves; en la semana 3, martes y viernes) y dos videos de LinkedIn con presentador IA, los miércoles 25 de noviembre y 2 de diciembre a las 11:00. Las semanas 1 y 2 ya están programadas con sus medios; las semanas 3 y 4 son borradores.

Blog en kleannyc.com

Propuesta. El blog del sitio ya existe, pero está detenido. Se retoma con un artículo cada dos semanas, cada uno sobre una sola búsqueda concreta y con un botón final para pedir presupuesto. Los posts de LinkedIn enlazan al artículo en lugar de a la página de contacto cuando haya uno relacionado. El efecto en Google tarda meses y no está garantizado.

Overview

Image prompts, texts and hashtags for Klean N Green NYC over four weeks.

Strategy: time back

The mix: about two pieces in three show the time, and one in three teaches and bridges.

The idea. Hiring Klean N Green NYC gives you time for what you could not fit in before. Every piece either shows what that time can become, or gives a useful tip and ends with a bridge to the service.

25pieces show what you can do with the time: a Saturday, a host's evening, a property manager's weekData
13pieces stay as tips and close with a bridge to the serviceData
15pieces are already scheduled with finished images or videos (weeks 1 and 2, plus the Nov 10 TikTok): only their text changesData
23pieces are drafts: new text, and new scenes, scripts and prompts where the idea changedData
AudienceWhat the time is forWhere it shows up
Families and homesWeekends, a slow breakfast, a walk, the call you keep postponingSaturday and Sunday posts, Reels, Google
HostsThe door, the drinks and the guests, not the spongeNov 18, Nov 27, Nov 28, TikTok
Rental hosts and landlordsGuest messages, showings and the listingGoogle, Nov 16
Property managersTenants, showings and inspections, instead of coordinating crewsLinkedIn: Nov 10, Nov 17, Dec 1, Dec 3
Office managers and teamsPeople, vendors, projects and the year-end closeLinkedIn: Nov 12 and Nov 24; Google, Nov 23
  • Lead with the moment, then the service. Open with what the person gets: a Saturday, a quiet evening, an open calendar. The service is how they get there.
  • Ask, do not claim. "What would you do with your Saturday?" invites the reader. The copy never says what clients did with their time: no invented clients, stories or testimonials. The only client voice is the Nov 19 Google review, word for word.
  • No hours-saved numbers. The numbers that remain (5 checks, 20 minutes, 30 minutes) are steps inside tips, not promises.
  • Tips stay, with a bridge. One piece in three is a tip. It ends with one bridge line from the bank below.
  • One call to action per piece: a free estimate, a message or a scope.
  • Scenes show the free time, not the work. People and Clover appear after the work is done: reading, a mug at the window, waiting for guests.
  • Weeks 1 and 2 keep their media. Those posts are scheduled with finished images and videos, so the proposal changes only the text. Tip carousels and Reels carry the bridge in the caption, and three carousels have an optional extra closing slide.
Bridge lines for tips (pick one per piece)
Prefer to spend that time elsewhere? We handle it. Message us for a free estimate.
Save the list, or hand it to us and keep the time.
Or let us take it off your list.
Want the whole day back? Message us for a free estimate.

Klean N Green NYC, Monday, November 9 to Sunday, December 6, 2026. All times are New York time.

Next step. Nothing in Metricool has changed. After you approve a piece I update it there: scheduled posts are edited in place, so their time and media stay, and drafts are replaced with the new text. A piece you do not approve stays as it is.

Brand look: palette, typography and layout

Source. These values come from the website's global settings. Every image prompt in this pack already carries them, so you do not need to add anything when you paste a prompt.

Primary green#2E7D32Main accent: check marks, thin rules and the call-to-action panel. White text on it passes contrast (5.1:1).
Bright green#4CB748Tiny decorative details only (accent line, dot, leaf). Never for text: 2.6:1 on white.
Ink#1E2A23Headlines and anything that must be read first (13.8:1 on the off-white).
Paragraph text#55645BBody copy and captions (5.8:1 on the off-white).
Slate#54595FSecondary labels and icons (6.5:1 on the off-white).
Background#F7F6F1Warm off-white: the text panel and any flat background.
Hover tint#DCEBD5Soft green for secondary shapes, chips and hairline dividers.
White#FFFFFFHighlights and text on green.

Green cleaning for New York homes

Recurring, one-time and move-out cleans, with a clear scope and a free estimate.

Headline: Cormorant Garamond bold, #1E2A23. Body: Jost, #55645B.

The website lists Jost and Cormorant Garamond without saying which is which. The prompts use Cormorant Garamond for headlines and Jost for body text and labels. If that is reversed, tell me and the prompts are updated.

  • Hierarchy. The headline is at least 1.8 times larger than the body text: cover 96 px or more, content slides about 64 px and 36 px. One idea per slide.
  • Contrast. Ink #1E2A23 on the off-white panel reads at 13.8:1, paragraph text #55645B at 5.8:1 and white on forest green at 5.1:1. Bright green #4CB748 is decoration only (2.6:1 on white) and never carries text.
  • Alignment and grid. Everything is left-aligned to one edge, inside a 90 px safe margin, and the panel sits in the same place on every slide so the set reads as one piece.
  • White space and proximity. Generous padding inside the panel; the accent line, headline and body are grouped tightly, with more space between that group and the photo.
  • Repetition and consistency. Same panel, same two fonts, same colors, same light and color grading across the set. The closing slide switches to a forest-green panel with white text as the end signal.
  • Focal point and rule of thirds. The subject of the photo sits on one third of the frame and at least half of the image stays visible as photo around the panel.
  • Color proportion (60-30-10). About 60% warm off-white and white, 30% photo tones and ink, 10% green.
Brand block that every image prompt includes
Brand colors, in this proportion: about 60% warm off-white #F7F6F1 and white #FFFFFF, about 30% the natural tones of the photo and dark ink #1E2A23, and about 10% green, with forest green #2E7D32 as the main accent and bright green #4CB748 only for tiny decorative details such as a short accent line. Body text color #55645B; soft green tint #DCEBD5 for secondary shapes and hairline dividers.

Typography: headline in Cormorant Garamond bold (an elegant high-contrast serif), color #1E2A23; body text and labels in Jost regular or medium (a clean geometric sans-serif), color #55645B.

Design: one text panel in warm off-white #F7F6F1 with 28 px rounded corners, between 45% and 80% of the frame height and inside a 90 px safe margin, with a 6 x 120 px bright-green #4CB748 accent line above the headline. Clear hierarchy: the headline at least 1.8 times larger than the body text. Everything left-aligned to one edge, generous white space inside the panel, large and legible on a phone. Compose the photo with the subject on one third of the frame and keep at least half of the image visible as photo around the panel.

Clover and a varied cast for single images

Applies to the single images: the Saturday posts, Wed Nov 18 and Sun Nov 29, each with a different person and Clover, and the optional Thanksgiving image, with Clover only. Carousels and Reels keep their layout and vary only the hands; LinkedIn images with no people stay as they are.

The idea. Single images carry no text: the caption says the message and the image is a photo. Clover, a gray tabby cat with a leaf-green collar, is the one recurring character; the people change from post to post in age, background and look, so the feed reflects the people who live in New York. Each prompt describes its own person, so no reference image is needed for the people. Generators also tend to garble text inside images, so removing it avoids the most common failure.

Step 1: Clover reference image (generate it first and attach it to every single-image prompt)
Photorealistic character reference image, vertical 4:5 format, 1080 x 1350 px, on a plain warm light-gray seamless studio background with soft, even daylight. One character, full body, sitting upright in a three-quarter view with space around it, no props: Clover, the cat. Clover, the cat: an adult gray tabby shorthair with a white chest, white front paws and a small white blaze on the nose, green eyes, upright ears and a calm, curious expression, wearing a thin leaf-green #2E7D32 collar with no tag. Keep the same markings, size and collar every time. Clover is a real-looking cat in a real photo: natural proportions and pose, no clothes, nothing held in its paws, no human gestures or expressions. Clover is always clean and well groomed, and never sits on food, dishes, a sink or freshly folded linens. No text, no logos, no people and no other animals.
Character block for Clover (already included in every single-image prompt)
Clover, the cat: an adult gray tabby shorthair with a white chest, white front paws and a small white blaze on the nose, green eyes, upright ears and a calm, curious expression, wearing a thin leaf-green #2E7D32 collar with no tag. Keep the same markings, size and collar every time. Clover is a real-looking cat in a real photo: natural proportions and pose, no clothes, nothing held in its paws, no human gestures or expressions. Clover is always clean and well groomed, and never sits on food, dishes, a sink or freshly folded linens.
  • Variety on purpose. The six people differ in age (mid-twenties to late sixties), gender and background, and they are residents in their own homes. For a new single image, choose someone who is not yet in the table.
  • Hands vary too. In carousels and Reels only hands appear. Each set uses a different skin tone and age, and the same hands stay through the whole set.
  • Same chat for the cat. Attach the Clover reference image to each prompt and check the markings and the collar before using each result. For the person, check the face, the hands (five fingers) and the clothes described in the prompt.
  • The people are fictional and AI-generated. Do not present them as real employees, customers or testimonials, and do not write captions as if they were on the team. The Instagram drafts are already marked as AI-generated.
  • No claims. Captions and images do not say anything about pets or pet-safe products unless the business confirms it first.
  • Clover stays out of the way of the work. Never on food, dishes, a sink or fresh linens; that is written into its block.
  • Real people instead? Use a photo of that person as the reference image, with their written permission, and replace the person block in that prompt. Real photos of the team and of jobs are always better than generated ones for Google and for the Dec 2 before and after.

Video prompts for Seedance

How it works. Each Reel is split into three scenes of 4 seconds. Generate them one by one in Seedance, then join and edit them in your video editor. The prompts do not ask for text, voice or music inside the video.

  • Aspect ratio 9:16 (vertical), the highest resolution the tool offers (1080 x 1920 px or above).
  • 4 seconds per scene, 3 scenes, about 12 seconds in total. Use 5 seconds per scene if you want more room to trim when editing.
  • Sound: ambient only (cloth on a surface, water). No music and no voice inside Seedance; add the voiceover (each Reel card has its script) and licensed music in the video editor.
  • Paste the continuity block at the start of every scene prompt, so location, light, props and the hand stay the same from scene to scene.
  • Generate and judge one scene at a time. If the tool offers it, use the last frame of the previous scene as the reference for the next one.
  • Change one line at a time: if the movement is right but the framing is off, edit only the Camera line; if the framing is right but the movement is off, edit only Subject and action.
Scene template (how every scene prompt is built)
Scene 1 of 3 of a vertical Reel titled "[Reel title]". 9:16, 4 seconds, one continuous shot.
Continuity: [continuity block: place, light, props, the same hand]
Subject and action: [one action, one sentence]
Camera: [one camera move: shot size, angle, movement]
Style: photorealistic live-action footage in the Klean N Green NYC look: bright and airy, natural color, warm off-white and white tones with leaf-green accents, 24 fps, smooth slow satisfying motion.
Sound: [ambient sound]
Constraints: only hands appear, no faces; no text, captions, subtitles or watermarks; no logos, no readable brand names or product labels; no cartoon, illustration or 3D-render look; natural, physically plausible hand and object movement; no cuts inside the scene.

Order inside each prompt: continuity, subject and action, camera, style, sound, constraints. One action and one camera move per scene keeps the motion clean. Each Reel card also has the three scenes as one labeled prompt (Shot 1, Shot 2, Shot 3) in case you prefer to generate the whole piece at once.

How to generate the images

  • One prompt generates one image. Each carousel has one prompt per slide, and each already carries the brand block (palette, typography and layout rules) and the exact text. Copy and paste one at a time.
  • Single images: one prompt, one finished image, no text on it. Each prompt includes the description of that image's person and of Clover, the recurring cat; attach the Clover reference image to every prompt so the cat stays the same (see "Clover and a varied cast for single images").
  • No slide numbers on the images. The prompts name the role of the slide (cover, content, closing) and tell the generator not to print counters, arrows or dots. The "Slide 3 of 7" labels in this pack are only for finding the prompt.
  • All slides of a carousel in the same chat, so light, panel and color grading stay identical from one slide to the next.
  • Check the spelling of the text in every image. If it comes out wrong, ask for another version, or keep only the photo: copy the sentence that follows "Photo scene:" in the prompt, generate the photo without text and set the text in Canva with Cormorant Garamond for the headline and Jost for the body, using the brand block in the Overview.
  • The logo is added afterward. Every prompt leaves a 160 x 160 px square empty at the bottom left, because generators do not reproduce a logo well.
  • Reels: one scene per prompt, for Seedance. Generate each 4-second scene separately with the continuity block, join them in a video editor and add on-screen text and music there. The "Video prompts for Seedance" section explains the settings.
  • Linked pages. Pieces with a link include the prompt for that page's featured image. Generate each page image once, upload it to the page and check the link preview before the piece goes out.
  • The prompts ask for photorealistic images. They are generic illustrative photos, not Klean's real work: do not present them as real jobs. Flag them as AI-generated so that option is set on Instagram when the drafts are created.

That image is also the preview card of the link, which LinkedIn shows only when the post has no image of its own. It is a website image: the posts themselves are 4:5, 1080 x 1350 px. Generate each page image once and reuse it.

Before approving: facts to confirm

  • Time-back wording. The copy says the service gives the reader time and asks what they would do with it. It promises no hours saved and never says what clients did with their time. Confirm you are comfortable with that line.
  • Weeks 1 and 2. Replacing the text of a scheduled post does not touch its image or video. Check that each new text still agrees with its media (the Nov 15 slides, for example, still say 20 minutes).
  • Dec 2 before and after. The text says "The client's time spent on it: none." Keep that line only if the client did nothing for that job, and fill in the bracketed details first.
  • Services named in the new copy. Recurring plans (weekly, every two weeks, monthly), turnover and vacation-rental cleans, a clean planned before guests arrive, and holiday scheduling for offices and property managers. Confirm each is offered as written.
  • Vacant-unit service for property managers. The LinkedIn texts talk about cleaning units between tenants. The website mentions vacation rentals and corporate housing, but not in those words.
  • Handover photos. The texts for Nov 10, Nov 17 and Dec 3 say photos of the key areas are taken at handover. If that is not the case, remove those lines.
  • Service areas. The Google post for Monday, Nov 9 mentions Manhattan, Brooklyn and Queens. The website also cites Jersey City and Hoboken.
  • Saturday posts. They invite people to send their dates and details for an estimate. Confirm that the team can reply to messages on weekends and in the first days of January.
  • /new-clients/ offer. Not used. If it is still valid, add it with an end date.
  • Customer review (Nov 19). It is a public Google review, quoted word for word and without the client's name. Confirm you are comfortable using it; if you prefer, ask the client first.
  • Real photos (Dec 2). That draft's text still contains bracketed placeholders: fill them with the real job before scheduling, and get the customer's authorization for the photos.
  • Thanksgiving post (Nov 26). New content, not in the original plan. Review the wording and decide whether it goes out.
  • Reel drafts. Instagram holds the logo as a placeholder because Metricool does not accept a Reel without a file. Remove the logo and upload the video before scheduling.
  • Brooklyn location. The Meta place "Brooklyn, New York" shows that name in the draft, but its stored map coordinates point to Colorado. If that matters, swap it for a Brooklyn business or neighborhood page.
  • Typography roles. The website lists Jost and Cormorant Garamond without saying which is which. The prompts use Cormorant Garamond for headlines and Jost for body text and labels; tell me if it is the other way round and I will swap them.
  • Google buttons in Metricool. Nov 9 and Nov 16 are scheduled with their images; Nov 23 and Nov 30 are text-only drafts. Metricool has no button field for Google, so the link sits in the text; the button (Book or Learn more) is only set if you publish directly in Google.
  • Phone number. No post text includes it, because Google can reject posts with a phone number in the text. The website footer shows (347) 833-4766 and the closing-slide design shows (347) 883-4766: confirm which one is correct before publishing.

TikTok with AI presenters

Posting times come from Metricool's general TikTok reference, because the account is new and has no history yet.

Format. Eight TikToks for Klean N Green NYC (@kleanandgreen), two per week, Tuesday and Thursday at 6 PM New York time (week 3 moves for Thanksgiving). Each one is a video of about 18 seconds in which a different AI-generated presenter talks to the camera. The topics are the time you get back (what would you do with your Saturday, what to ask a provider) and practical cleaning tips that end with a bridge to the service. The scripts, prompts and captions are inside each week, among the other pieces.

8TikToks for Klean N Green NYC, each with its own presenter, script, video prompt and captionData
7drafts saved in Metricool, with a placeholder photo until you add the video (the Nov 10 TikTok is scheduled with its video)Data
18 sabout, per video: 3 beats of 35 to 47 wordsData

TikTok: how each video is made

  • 1. Generate. Copy the video prompt in Seedance or a similar tool. If it does not produce a voice with accurate lip sync, record the script in ElevenLabs and apply lip sync to the video.
  • 2. Clips. If your generator caps a clip below 18 seconds, generate beats 1 and 2 as one clip and beat 3 as another, with the same presenter and setting, and join them in the editor.
  • 3. Check. The face, hands and mouth look natural, the words match the script, and nothing on screen is garbled. Regenerate otherwise.
  • 4. Edit. Add the on-screen text and subtitles in the TikTok or video editor. Generators garble text inside video, so the prompts ask for none.
  • 5. Upload. Open the Metricool draft, remove the placeholder photo, upload the video and switch on the AI-generated content label.
  • 6. Schedule. Only after you have checked it. Nothing here is published without your approval.

TikTok: presenter rules

  • A different presenter in every video. Different ages, skin tones and settings. No presenter returns, and none should resemble a real person.
  • The presenter is a host, not a customer or an employee. The scripts give tips and information. None says "I shop here" or "I have been cleaning for years", because an invented person cannot give a real testimonial.
  • Always labeled as AI. Every caption ends with "AI-generated video." and the TikTok AI-generated content label must be on when the video is uploaded.
  • Avoid in every prompt: subtitles, logos, watermarks, music, extra people, readable brand names and text on screen.

TikTok: before approving

  • Claims. The tips are general and safe. No script promises results, certifications or product performance.
  • Safety tip (Nov 19). Never mixing bleach with ammonia or vinegar is standard guidance. Keep the line as written.
  • Contact. The captions invite a message and give no phone number, because the number differs between sources. Confirm the number before adding one.
  • Friday, November 27. Week 3 uses Tuesday and Friday because Thursday the 26th is Thanksgiving.
  • Services mentioned. Turnover cleaning and property management appear in the Klean N Green NYC pack. Confirm the company offers what the scripts imply.
  • Presenters. The Klean rule of varying the people in the images also applies here: every TikTok has a different presenter.
  • Commercial content label. Metricool left TikTok's "your brand" option off. Decide when scheduling whether to turn it on.

Turnover week: 5 checks

LinkedIn · Text with image Time back Scheduled · text change proposed

Angle: Property managers: turnover week without the chase.

Status and media

Proposed text (replaces the scheduled one)
LinkedIn text
Turnover week should not take over your week.

Between tenants, a unit needs five checks:
1. Kitchen: inside the oven, fridge and cabinets.
2. Bathrooms: grout, fixtures and exhaust fans.
3. Windows: glass, tracks and sills.
4. Floors: edges and corners, not only the center.
5. Final walkthrough with photos.

Done by you, that is a list to run and re-run. Done by us, it is a handover with photos, and your hours go to tenants, showings and the next unit.

Property managers in NYC: tell us how many units you turn over each month and we will scope it. Request a free estimate: https://kleannyc.com/contact-us/?utm_source=linkedin&utm_medium=social&utm_campaign=nov2026_turnover

#NYCCleaning #PropertyManagement #CommercialCleaning

1. Kitchen: inside the oven, fridge and cabinets.

What office cleaning includes

LinkedIn · Text with image Time back Scheduled · text change proposed

Angle: Office managers: the team's time goes to the work.

Status and media
  • Already scheduled in Metricool with its finished media. Nothing has been changed there.
  • Image unchanged: the post is scheduled with its image, and the new text works with it.

Proposed text (replaces the scheduled one)
LinkedIn text
What does office cleaning actually include? And what does it free up?

The scope changes with the space, but a regular office clean usually covers:
• Desks and shared surfaces, including high-touch points like handles and switches
• Kitchenette and break room
• Restrooms, cleaned and restocked
• Trash and recycling
• Floors, vacuumed and mopped
• Entrance, glass and common areas

That is a list nobody on your team should have to think about. When it is handled, office managers spend their time on people, vendors and projects, and everyone else on their actual work.

The right frequency depends on headcount and how the space is used. A 3-person studio and a 40-person floor need different plans.

Message us the size of your space and how often you would like service, and we will send a scope.

#NYCCleaning #OfficeCleaning #FacilitiesManagement

Tue, Nov 2411 AM

Year-end office checklist

LinkedIn · 8-slide carousel Tip + bridge Draft · add image Draft · new text proposed

Angle: Checklist first. The bridge is the last slide and the last paragraph.

Text and hashtags
LinkedIn text
Before the office closes for the holidays: a 6-point cleaning checklist. Swipe through.

1. Clear and wipe desks, so nothing sits for two weeks.
2. Empty the break room fridge and microwave.
3. Restrooms: deep clean and restock.
4. Trash and recycling: empty everything and replace liners.
5. Floors: vacuum and mop, including corners and under desks.
6. Glass, entrance and high-touch points.

Coming back to a clean office on the first Monday of January sets the tone for the quarter. If your last week is already full, hand the list to us and let your team spend it closing the year.

Reel: Guests leave. Your afternoon stays yours

Instagram and Facebook · Reel, about 12 seconds Time back Draft · add video Draft · new text proposed

Angle: Guests leave, the reset is handled and the afternoon is yours.

Metricool draft: what is left to do
  • Saved in Metricool as an Instagram Reel draft for 12 PM, with the caption, hashtags and Brooklyn location. It carries the logo as a placeholder image, because Metricool does not accept a Reel without a file.
  • To finish it: open the draft, remove the logo image and upload the video. The type is already Reel; the "Images are not supported" warning disappears once the video replaces the logo.
  • The Facebook draft is text only, with the Brooklyn location. Add the same video there and, if you want it as a Reel, change its type to Reel.
  • The Instagram draft is already marked as AI-generated content.
What the video needs
  • Vertical 9:16, 1080 x 1920 px. 3 scenes of 4 seconds each, about 12 seconds in total.
  • Add the on-screen text, the voiceover (script below) and licensed music in the video editor: generators garble text inside video.
  • Cover: use a frame from scene 1 and add the title in Canva, in Cormorant Garamond bold on the off-white #F7F6F1.
  • The settings and the way the prompts are built are explained in the Overview, under "Video prompts for Seedance".
Continuity block: paste it at the start of every scene
Continuity block
A bright New York guest room with white walls, a wooden nightstand with a small lamp, and a window with sheer curtains. Soft morning daylight at about 5500 K. Props: white linens, a stack of folded white towels, a small green plant on the nightstand, a damp leaf-green microfiber cloth, an open book with no readable text and a leaf-green ceramic mug on the windowsill. Palette: warm whites, natural wood and leaf green. The same hands in every scene: adult hands with deep brown skin, short natural nails, no jewelry, no watch.
Seedance prompts: 3 scenes, one prompt per scene
Scene 1 of 3: Guests just left
Scene 1 of 3 of a vertical Reel titled "Guests leave. Your afternoon stays yours". 9:16, 4 seconds, one continuous shot.
Continuity: a bright New York guest room with white walls, a wooden nightstand with a small lamp, and a window with sheer curtains. Soft morning daylight at about 5500 K. Props: white linens, a stack of folded white towels, a small green plant on the nightstand, a damp leaf-green microfiber cloth, an open book with no readable text and a leaf-green ceramic mug on the windowsill. Palette: warm whites, natural wood and leaf green. The same hands in every scene: adult hands with deep brown skin, short natural nails, no jewelry, no watch.
Subject and action: Two hands pull the used sheets off a bed in one smooth motion and gather them into a bundle.
Camera: medium shot from the foot of the bed at a slightly high angle, locked off.
Style: photorealistic live-action footage in the Klean N Green NYC look: bright and airy, natural color, warm off-white and white tones with leaf-green accents, 24 fps, smooth slow satisfying motion.
Sound: a soft rustle of fabric.
Constraints: only hands appear, no faces; no text, captions, subtitles or watermarks; no logos, no readable brand names or product labels; no cartoon, illustration or 3D-render look; natural, physically plausible hand and object movement; no cuts inside the scene.

Continuity: a bright New York guest room with white walls, a wooden nightstand with a small lamp, and a window with sheer curtains. Soft morning daylight at about 5500 K. Props: white linens, a stack of folded white towels, a small green plant on the nightstand, a damp leaf-green microfiber cloth, an open book with no readable text and a leaf-green ceramic mug on the windowsill. Shot 1 (4 s): Two hands pull the used sheets off a bed in one smooth motion and gather them into a bundle. Camera: medium shot from the foot of the bed at a slightly high angle, locked off. Sound: a soft rustle of fabric.
Shot 2 (4 s): The same room appears finished: the bed is made with fresh white sheets, folded white towels sit at its foot, the nightstand is wiped and the lamp is in place; nothing moves except a slow drift of light. Camera: wide shot from the doorway, locked off with a very slow push-in of about 5%. Sound: quiet room tone.
Shot 3 (4 s): A hand lifts a leaf-green mug from the windowsill beside an open book and holds it, steam rising; the made bed is softly blurred behind. Camera: medium close-up on the windowsill, slow rack focus from the mug to the made bed. Sound: a soft ceramic touch, then calm quiet.
Style: photorealistic live-action footage in the Klean N Green NYC look: bright and airy, natural color, warm off-white and white tones with leaf-green accents, 24 fps, smooth slow satisfying motion.
Constraints: only hands appear, no faces; no text, captions, subtitles or watermarks; no logos, no readable brand names or product labels; no cartoon, illustration or 3D-render look; natural, physically plausible hand and object movement; no cuts inside the scene.
On-screen text by scene
On-screen text (add in the video editor)
Scene 1: Guests just left
Scene 2: The reset is handled
Scene 3: Your afternoon is yours · Send this to someone who hosts
Voiceover script (audio)
  • About 24 words for 12 seconds: relaxed pace, with a short pause between scenes. If it feels rushed, drop the first sentence of the scene.
  • The first line is the hook: say it like a question to a friend, within the first two seconds. Smile on the last line, the call to action.
  • Tone: talk, don't read. Like telling a friend something you just noticed, in casual American English, with contractions and a little humor.
Voiceover by scene, with timing
0-4 s  Scene 1: Guests just left. And there's the bed.
4-8 s  Scene 2: Now imagine the whole room, already reset.
8-12 s  Scene 3: And your afternoon is yours! Send this to a host.
  • Model: Eleven v4. The tags in square brackets are directions, not spoken text. Paste the whole block as it is.
  • A tag holds until the next one, so tags change only where the delivery changes. One emotion per phrase works best.
  • Generate the three lines as one take so the tone stays continuous, then cut it to the scenes in the video editor.
  • If a line does not land, change the tag instead of rewriting the words: [warmly], [excited], [curious], [softly], [chuckles], [short pause]. Capitals mark emphasis, and "..." a longer beat.
  • Voice matters as much as the text: pick a warm, natural American voice before iterating. Keep the phone number and web address out of the voiceover.
Script with audio tags for Eleven v4 (paste into ElevenLabs)
[sighs] Guests just left. [short pause] And there's the bed.
[warmly] Now imagine the whole room, already reset.
[excited] And your afternoon is yours! Send this to a host.
Plain script, without tags, to record yourself
Guests just left. And there's the bed. Now imagine the whole room, already reset. And your afternoon is yours! Send this to a host.

What property management cleaning includes

Angle: Property managers: time for tenants, not for coordinating crews.

Text and hashtags
LinkedIn text
What does cleaning for property management include? And what does it give back? Swipe through.

• Turnover cleans for vacant units between tenants
• Vacation rental and corporate housing cleans between guests
• Post-renovation cleaning before a unit goes back on the market
• Photos of the key areas at handover

Every building is different. Tell us the type of property and the number of units, and we will put together a plan, so your time goes to your tenants and not to coordinating crews.

Request a free estimate: https://kleannyc.com/professional-cleaning-service-in-brooklyn/?utm_source=linkedin&utm_medium=social&utm_campaign=dic2026_administracion

Style: photorealistic live-action footage in the Klean N Green NYC look: bright and airy, natural color, warm off-white and white tones with leaf-green accents, 24 fps, smooth slow satisfying motion.
Sound: a soft wipe, then the small click of the remote on wood.

On-screen text (add in the video editor)
Scene 1: Before the holiday visits · 1. Door handles
Scene 2: 2. Light switches
Scene 3: 3. Remote controls · Or let us do it
Voiceover script (audio)
  • About 27 words for 12 seconds: relaxed pace, with a short pause between scenes. If it feels rushed, drop the first sentence of the scene.
  • The first line is the hook: say it like a question to a friend, within the first two seconds. Smile on the last line, the call to action.
  • Tone: talk, don't read. Like telling a friend something you just noticed, in casual American English, with contractions and a little humor.
Voiceover by scene, with timing
0-4 s  Scene 1: What's the first thing guests touch? Your door handle.
4-8 s  Scene 2: Then the light switch. Everyone taps it.
8-12 s  Scene 3: And the remote! Wipe all three, or let us do it.
  • Model: Eleven v4. The tags in square brackets are directions, not spoken text. Paste the whole block as it is.
  • A tag holds until the next one, so tags change only where the delivery changes. One emotion per phrase works best.
  • Generate the three lines as one take so the tone stays continuous, then cut it to the scenes in the video editor.
  • If a line does not land, change the tag instead of rewriting the words: [warmly], [excited], [curious], [softly], [chuckles], [short pause]. Capitals mark emphasis, and "..." a longer beat.
  • Voice matters as much as the text: pick a warm, natural American voice before iterating. Keep the phone number and web address out of the voiceover.

Photorealistic photograph, not an illustration, for a social media post by Klean N Green NYC, a green cleaning company in New York City. Vertical 4:5 format, 1080 x 1350 px. Clover is always clean and well groomed, and never sits on food, dishes, a sink or freshly folded linens. No other animals and no other people. Cómo usarlo. Elige la marca arriba y luego una pestaña. Los botones «Copiar» y «Copy» copian el texto de cada caja. Las horas son de Nueva York, salvo las de Klean Perú, que son de Lima.

Las etiquetas Referencia e Hipótesis tienen el mismo significado que en la página de estrategia. Los datos entre corchetes se completan con información real de cada negocio. Salvo el ají amarillo y la chicha morada, que ya aparecen en publicaciones de la cuenta, los productos y platos se confirman con la tienda.

Los posts en inglés salen de la estrategia y del calendario de cada marca. Lo que depende del negocio está en «Before approving» de cada Overview. Las semanas 5 a 13 se detallan día por día en cada revisión. JDET Capital y Klean Perú dependen de las definiciones de la semana 1. No se publica nada sin aprobación.
